What if I am lost in the alcoves of the hills, if I vanish forever in the night? The sun that sets must rise. It is rising and lighting up the dark and distant continents even when setting. Think of that, ye who gloat over the sinking of my mortal self.

“No; an idea is never too early annunciated. The good seed will grow among the rocks, and though the heavens withhold from it the sunshine and rain. It is because I will it, nay, because a higher Will than mine wills it, that the spirit of Khalid shall yet flow among your pilgrim caravans, through the fertile deserts of Arabia, down to the fountainhead of Faith, to Mecca and Medina,” et cetera.

===Chinese===
「为真理而受苦，其中那一缕微妙的欢愉之颤栗，究竟从何而来？」哈立德问道。「从殉道者的伤口里流出的那一缕光，从何而来？那战胜了他们痛楚的狂喜，又从何而来？在夜色浓重之处，穿过群山的幽壑，越过它们荒芜的峰巅，再顺着那条名为忘却的干河谷（wadi）而下——他们悄然走过。而且他们做梦。他们梦见在消逝中的显现；在降服中的凯旋；在夕阳里的日出。

「一道磅礴的大潮，会在海滩高处留下一道痕迹，这痕迹日后便成了海洋的一般水位。世间的伟大思想家——诗人与先知、智者与强者与克己者、「人」之宗教的宣告者——亦是如此。而我的兄弟们哪，我不过是这片巨木之林中的一株矮栎（scrub-oak）。一株你可以砍倒、却拔不起的矮栎。剪去我的枝条罢；把斧子砍上我的躯干罢；把我的木材烧成炭，放进你们礼拜之庙的香炉里罢；可我的根扎得深，深深地扎在土壤里，非凡人之手所能触及。它们甚至正在你们那些摇摇欲坠的宫殿与庙宇底下蔓延。……

「我梦见东方的觉醒；梦见强盛的东方诸民族奋起，去荣耀那个理念，去为那「普遍之灵」建造庙宇——为艺术、为爱、为真理、为信仰。纵使我迷失在群山的幽壑里，纵使我永远消逝于这黑夜之中，又当如何？落下去的太阳终将再升。即便在它落下之时，它也正在升起，正照亮那些幽暗而遥远的大陆。我那凡躯之沉沦，你们这些幸灾乐祸的人哪，且想一想这个罢。

「不；一个理念，宣告出来永远不会嫌太早。好种子即便在岩石之间、即便上天吝它的阳光与雨水，也会生长。正因为我要它如此——不，因为一个比我更高的意志要它如此——哈立德之灵终将流贯你们的朝圣驼队，穿过阿拉伯那些丰饶的沙漠，一直流到信仰的本源，流到麦加（Mecca）与麦地那（Medina），」云云。

===Original===
This, perhaps the last of the rhapsodies of Khalid’s, the Reader considering the circumstances under which it was written, will no doubt condone. Further, however, in the K.L. MS. we can not now proceed. Certainly the Author is not wanting in the sort of courage which is loud-lunged behind the writing table; his sufficiency of spirit is remarkable, unutterable. But we would he knew that the strong do not exult in their strength, nor the wise in their wisdom. For to fly and philosophize were one thing, and to philosophize in prison were another. Khalid this time does not follow closely in the way of the Masters. But he would have done so, if we can believe Shakib in this, had not Mrs. Gotfry persuaded him to the contrary. He would have stood in the Turkish Areopagus at Constantinople, defended himself somewhat Socratic before his judges, and hung out his tung on a rickety gibbet in the neighborhood of St. Sophia. But Mrs. Gotfry spoiled his great chance. She cheated him of the glory of dying for a noble cause.

===Chinese===
这一段——大概是哈立德最后的一篇狂想了——读者念及它写下时所处的境况，想必会加以宽宥。然而，赫迪夫图书馆手稿（K.L. MS.）往下我们此刻是不能再走了。诚然，作者并不缺乏那种在写字台后扯开嗓门逞勇的胆气；他那股子精神的充沛，倒是可惊、不可名状的。但我们倒愿意他明白：强者并不以其力自矜，智者亦不以其智自矜。因为一边逃亡一边高谈哲理是一回事，在牢狱里高谈哲理又是另一回事。这一回，哈立德并未紧随诸位大师的脚步。然而倘若我们在此能信得过沙基布的话，他本来是会那样做的——要不是戈特弗里夫人（Mrs. Gotfry）劝阻了他。他本可以站在君士坦丁堡那座土耳其式的战神山议事庭（Areopagus）上，在他那些法官面前作一番多少带苏格拉底气味的申辩，而后在圣索菲亚（St. Sophia）近旁的一副摇摇晃晃的绞架上，把他那条舌头挂出来。可戈特弗里夫人毁了他这千载难逢的良机。她骗走了他为高尚事业而死的荣光。

===Original===
“The Turks are not worth the sacrifice,” Shakib heard her say, when Khalid ejaculated somewhat about martyrdom. And when she offered to accompany him, the flight did not seem shameful in his eyes. Nay, it became necessary; and under the circumstances it was, indeed, cowardice not to fly. For is it not as noble to surrender one’s self to Love as to the Turks or any other earthly despotism? Gladly, heroically, he adventures forth, therefore, and philosophizes on the way about the light that flows from the wounds of persecution. But we regret that this celestial stream is not unmixed; it is accompanied by blood and pus; by distention and fever, and other inward and outward sores.

===Chinese===
「土耳其人不值得你作这样的牺牲，」——当哈立德脱口说出几句关于殉道的话时，沙基布听见她这样说。而当她提出愿意陪他同行，逃亡在他眼里便算不得可耻了。不但如此，它简直成了势所必行；照那情形，不逃反倒是懦怯了。因为，把自己献给「爱」，难道不同把己身献给土耳其人、献给任何世俗的专制一样高贵么？于是他欣然、英勇地踏上前路，一路之上还不忘发议论，谈论那从迫害之伤口里流出来的光。只是我们遗憾地说，这道天上的溪流并不纯净；它夹杂着血与脓；夹杂着鼓胀与高烧，以及种种内内外外的疮伤。

===Original===
In this grievous state, somewhat like Don Quixote after the Battle of the Mill, our Khalid enters Baalbek. If the reader likes the comparison between the two Knights at this juncture, he must work it out for himself. We can not be so uncharitable as that; especially that our Knight is a compatriot, and is now, after our weary journeyings together, become our friend.﻿—Our poor grievous friend who must submit again to the surgeon’s knife.

===Chinese===
便是在这副伤痛的光景里——颇像堂吉诃德（Don Quixote）在磨坊之战后的模样——我们的哈立德进了巴勒贝克（Baalbek）。若读者喜欢在此关头把这两位骑士作一比较，那便请自去推求罢。我们可狠不下那样的心肠；况且我们的这位骑士又是同胞，而经过我们一路同行的劳顿之后，他已成为我们的朋友。——我们这位可怜的、伤痛的朋友，他必须再一次把自己交给外科医生的刀了。

===Original===
Mrs. Gotfry would not let him go to his mother, for she herself would nurse him. So, the doctor is called to the Hotel. And after opening, disinfecting, and dressing the wounds, he orders his patient to keep in bed for some days. They will then visit the ruins and resume their journeying to Egypt. Khalid no longer would live in Syria﻿—in a country forever doomed to be under the Turkish yoke, faring, nay, misfaring alike in the New Era as in the Old.

===Chinese===
戈特弗里夫人不让他回到母亲身边去，因为她要亲自照护他。于是医生被请到了旅馆。开刀、消毒、裹伤之后，医生吩咐病人卧床数日。此后他们再去游览那片废墟，然后继续他们赴埃及的旅程。哈立德再也不愿在叙利亚（Syria）生活了——不愿在一片永远注定要受土耳其轭所压的国度里生活，无论在新时代抑或旧时代，它都一样地得过且过——不，一样地不得善过。

===Original===
Now, his mother, tottering with age and sorrow, comes to the Hotel, and begs him in a flood of tears to come home; for his father is now with the Jesuits of Beirut and seldom comes to Baalbek. And his cousin Najma, with a babe on her arm and a tale of woe in her eyes, comes also to invite her cousin Khalid to her house.

===Chinese===
这时候，他母亲——因年迈与哀伤而步履蹒跚——来到了旅馆，泪流满面地求他回家；因为他的父亲如今与贝鲁特（Beirut）的耶稣会士（Jesuits）在一起，很少回到巴勒贝克来。而他的表妹娜吉玛（Najma），臂上抱着一个婴孩，眼里含着一腔苦楚，也来邀请她表哥哈立德到她家里去。

===Original===
She is alone; her father died some months ago; her husband, after the dethronement of Abdul Hamid, being implicated in the reaction-movement, fled the country; and his relatives, to add to her affliction, would deprive her of her child. She is alone; and sick in the lungs. She coughs, too, the same sharp, dry, malignant cough that once plagued Khalid. Ay, the same disease which he buried in the pine forest of Mt. Lebanon, he beholds the ghost of it now, more terrible and heartrending than anything he has yet seen or experienced. The disease which he conquered is come back in the person of his cousin Najma to conquer him. And who can assure Khalid that it did not steal into her breast along with his kisses? And yet, he is not the only one in Baalbek who returned from America with phthisis. O, but that thought is horrifying. Impossible﻿—he can not believe it.

===Chinese===
她形单影只；父亲数月前已去世；丈夫在阿卜杜勒·哈米德（Abdul Hamid）被废之后，因牵连于那场反动风潮，已逃往他乡；而夫家亲族为加重她的苦楚，竟要夺走她的孩子。她形单影只；而且肺里有病。她咳嗽起来，也是哈立德当年所苦的那种尖锐、干涩、凶险的咳。是啊，他当年埋葬在黎巴嫩山（Mt. Lebanon）松林里的那同一种病，如今他却看见了它的鬼魂——比他所见过、所经历过的一切都更为可怖、更为揪心。他曾经征服过的那病，如今借着表妹娜吉玛之身回过头来征服他。而谁能向哈立德保证，它不是随着他的亲吻一同潜入她的胸中呢？然而，他并不是巴勒贝克唯一一个从美洲带着肺痨（phthisis）回来的人。啊，可这个念头太骇人了。断无可能——他不能相信。

===Original===
But whether it be from you or from another, O Khalid, there is the ghost of it beckoning to you. Look at it. Are those the cheeks, those the eyes, this the body which a year ago was a model of rural charm and beauty and health? Is this the compensation of love? Is there anything like it dreamt of in your philosophy? There she is, who once in the ruined Temple of Venus mixed the pomegranate flower of her cheeks with the saffron of thy sickly lips. Wasted and dejected, broken in body and spirit, she sits by your bedside nursing her baby and coughing all the while. And that fixed expression of sadness, so habitual among the Arab women who carry their punks and their children on their backs and go a-begging, it seems as if it were an hundred autumns old, this sadness. But right there, only a year ago, the crimson poppies dallied with the laughing breeze; the melting rubies dilated of health and joy.

===Chinese===
然而哈立德啊，无论它是从你而来、还是从别人而来，那鬼魂就在那里向你招手。且看它罢。这难道是一年前的那些脸颊、那些眼睛、那副身子么——一年前，它们还是乡野的风姿、之美、之康健的典范。难道这便是爱的报酬？你的哲学里可曾梦见过这等光景？她便是那个人——从前在维纳斯神庙（Temple of Venus）的废墟里，她曾把她双颊的石榴花，搀进你那病唇的藏红花里去。如今消瘦而颓丧，身与心俱已破碎，她坐在你床边哺着她的婴儿，一路咳个不住。而那一副凝定的愁容——在那些背着水烟壶、背着孩子沿门乞讨的阿拉伯妇人中间那么习见——这份愁情，竟仿佛已历过一百个秋天。可在那同一个地方，仅仅一年以前，绯红的罂粟还正与含笑的微风调笑；那融融的红玉里，还鼓胀着康健与欢欣。

===Original===
And now, deploring, imploring, she asks: “Will you not come to me, O Khalid? Will you not let me nurse you? Come; and your mother, too, will live with us. I am so lonesome, so miserable. And at night the boys cast stones at my door. My husband’s relatives put them to it because I would not give them the child. And they circulate all kinds of calumnies about me too.”

Khalid promises to come, and assures her that she will not long remain alone. “And Allah willing,” he adds, “you will recover and be happy again.”

She rises to go, when Mrs. Gotfry enters the room. Khalid introduces his cousin as his dead bride. “What do you mean?” she inquires. He promises to explain. Meanwhile, she goes to her room, brings some sweetmeats in a round box inlaid with mother-of-pearl for Khalid’s guests. And taking the babe in her arms, she fondles and kisses it, and gives its mother some advice about suckling. “Not whenever the child cries, but only at stated times,” she repeats.

===Chinese===
如今，她悲叹着、恳求着，问道：「哈立德啊，你不愿意到我这里来么？你不愿让我来照护你么？来罢；你母亲也来与我们同住。我是这样孤单，这样凄苦。夜里，那些男孩子还朝我家门口扔石头。是我丈夫那些亲族支使他们这么干的，因为我不肯把孩子交给他们。他们还四处散布关于我的种种流言蜚语。」

哈立德答应去，并向她保证，她不会久处于孤寂之中。「而且真主（Allah）若愿意，」他补充道，「你会康复，会重新快活起来的。」

她起身要走，这时戈特弗里夫人走进房来。哈立德把表妹介绍为他的亡妻。「你这话是什么意思？」夫人问道。他答应稍后解释。其间，夫人回到自己房里，取来一盒甜食——装在一只螺钿镶嵌的圆盒里——招待哈立德的客人。她又把婴儿抱在怀里，抚弄着、亲吻着，并向孩子的母亲叮嘱了几句哺乳之道。「不是孩子一哭就喂，而是要按时喂，」她反复说道。

===Original===
So much about Khalid’s mother and cousin. A few days after, when he is able to leave his room, he goes to see them. His cousin Najma he would take with him to Cairo. He would not leave her behind, a prey to the cruelty of loneliness and disease. He tells her this. She is overjoyed. She is ready to go whenever he says. Tomorrow? Please Allah, yes. But﻿—

Please Allah, ill-luck is following. For on his way back to the Hotel, a knot of boys, lying in wait in one of the side streets, cast stones at him. He looks back, and a missile whizzes above his head, another hits him in the forehead almost undoing the doctor’s work. Alas, that wound! Will it ever heal? Khalid takes shelter in one of the shops; a cameleer rates the boys and chases them away. The stoning was repeated the following day, and the cause of it, Shakib tells us, is patent. For when it became known in Baalbek that Khalid, the excommunicated one, is living in the Hotel, and with an American woman! the old prejudices against him were aroused, the old enemies were astirring. The priests held up their hands in horror; the women wagged their long tongues in the puddle of scandal; and the most fanatical shrieked out, execrating, vituperating, threatening even the respectable Shakib, who persists in befriending this muleteer’s son. Excommunicated, he now comes with this *Americaniyah* (American woman) to corrupt the community. Horrible! We will even go farther than this boy’s play of stoning. We present petitions to the *kaimakam* demanding the expulsion of this Khalid from the Hotel, from the City.

===Chinese===
关于哈立德的母亲与表妹，且说到此处。数日之后，当他能下床出门了，便去看望她们。表妹娜吉玛，他要带她一同去开罗（Cairo）。他不愿把她留下，让她沦为孤独与疾病那般残酷的猎物。他把这话告诉她。她喜出望外。他说何时走，她便何时成行。明日？托真主的福，可以的。可是——

托真主的福，厄运却紧随而来。因为在他回旅馆的路上，一伙男孩子埋伏在一条小巷里，朝他投石子。他回头一看，一枚飞弹从他头顶嗖地飞过，另一枚正中他前额，险些把医生的活儿前功尽弃。唉，那道伤口！它可还有愈合的一天么？哈立德躲进一间铺子避难；一个赶骆驼的人把那群男孩子痛骂了一顿，赶散了他们。第二天的投石又重演了一次，而其中原委，据沙基布说，是昭然若揭的。因为巴勒贝克城里一经传开，说那个被逐出教门的哈立德，竟住在旅馆里，而且与一个美国女人同居！从前对他的种种旧偏见便被煽起，那些旧仇敌也骚动起来。神父们惊骇地高举双手；妇人们则在流言的泥潭里拖着她们长长的舌头；最狂热的那一伙尖声叫嚷着，咒骂、詈骂、甚至威胁起那位体面的沙基布来——他竟执意要与这个赶骡人之子交好。他既已被逐出教门，如今又带着这个「阿美利加尼娅」（Americaniyah，即美国女人）来腐蚀这地方的民风。可恨！我们还要比这群男孩的投石把戏更进一步。我们要向「卡伊马卡姆」（kaimakam，即县长）呈递请愿书，要求把这个哈立德从旅馆、从这座城里驱逐出去。

===Original===
From other quarters, however, come heavier charges against Khalid. The Government of Damascus has not been idle ever since the seditious lack-beard Sheikh disappeared. The telegraph wires, in all the principal cities of Syria, are vibrating with inquiries about him, with orders for his arrest. One such the *kaimakam* of Baalbek had just received when the petition of the “Guardians of the Morals of the Community” was presented to him. To this, the *kaimakam*, in a perfunctory manner, applies his seal, and assures his petitioners that it will promptly be turned over to the proper official. But Turk as Turks go, he “places it under the cushion,” when they leave. Which expression, translated into English means, he quashes it.

===Chinese===
然而，从别的方向还传来对哈立德更重的指控。自那位煽惑人心的「缺须谢赫」（Sheikh）失踪以来，大马士革（Damascus）政府并未闲着。叙利亚各大城市的电报线，都因打探他下落的询电、因逮捕他的命令而嗡嗡震动。巴勒贝克的「卡伊马卡姆」刚刚接到这样一份电报，「民风守护者们」的请愿书便也递到了他面前。对此，那位「卡伊马卡姆」敷衍了事地盖了印，并向请愿人保证，定将迅速移交主管官署。可他到底是个地道的土耳其人——他们一离去，他便「把它塞到垫子底下」。这一句话译成英文，便是：他把它压下了、搁置了。

===Original===
Now, by good chance, this is the same *kaimakam* who sent Khalid a year ago to prison, maugre the efforts and importunities and other inducements of Shakib. And this time, he will do him and his friend a good turn. He was thinking of the many misfortunes of this Khalid, and nursing a little pity for him, when Shakib entered to offer a written complaint against a few of the more noted instigators of the assailants of his friend. His Excellency puts this in his pocket and withdraws with Shakib into another room. A few minutes after, Shakib was hurrying to the Hotel to confer with his brother Khalid and Mrs. Gotfry.

“I saw the Order with these very eyes,” said Shakib, almost poking his two forefingers into them. “The *kaimakam* showed it to me.”

===Chinese===
巧得很，这位「卡伊马卡姆」，正是一年前不顾沙基布的种种奔走、苦求与其他打点，把哈立德投进牢狱的那一位。可这一回，他却要替他和他的朋友做一件好事了。他正想着这个哈立德所遭的种种不幸、心里对他生出一丝怜悯之时，沙基布进来了——递上一份书面诉状，控告几个带头袭击他朋友的首恶分子。大人把诉状揣进口袋，随沙基布退入另一间房里。几分钟后，沙基布便匆匆赶回旅馆，与他的兄弟哈立德及戈特弗里夫人密议。

「我亲眼看见那道命令的，」沙基布说着，几乎要把两个食指戳到自己眼睛里去。「是那位『卡伊马卡姆』亲口拿给我看的。」

===Original===
Hence, the secret preparations inside the Hotel and out of it for a second remove, for a final flight. Shakib packs up; Najma is all ready. And Khalid cuts his hair, doffs his jubbah, and appears again in the ordinary attire of civilised mortals. For how else can he get out of Beirut and the telegraph wires throughout Syria are flowing with orders for his arrest? In a hat and frock-coat, therefore (furnished by Shakib), he enters into the carriage with Mrs. Gotfry about two hours after midnight; and, with their whole retinue, make for Riak, and thence by train for Beirut. Here Shakib obtains passports for himself and Najma, and together with Mrs. Gotfry and her dragoman, they board in the afternoon the Austrian Liner for Port-Said; while, in the evening, walking at the side of one of the boatmen, Khalid, passportless, stealthily passes through the port, and rejoins his friends.

===Chinese===
于是，旅馆里里外外便开始了第二次搬迁的秘密筹备——为最终的出逃。沙基布收拾行装；娜吉玛一切就绪。而哈立德也剪短了头发，脱下长袍（jubbah），重新换上了文明凡人的寻常装束。因为不然的话，他又怎能出得了贝鲁特？要知道全叙利亚的电报线，正川流着缉拿他的命令呢。于是，他戴着帽子、穿着礼服大衣（这两样都是沙基布供给的），在午夜过后约两个钟头，与戈特弗里夫人一同钻进马车；一行人马全数随行，直奔里亚克（Riak），再从那里乘火车去贝鲁特。在这里，沙基布为自己和娜吉玛领到了护照，又与戈特弗里夫人及其口译向导（dragoman）一道，于午后登上开往塞得港（Port-Said）的奥地利邮轮；而到了傍晚，哈立德则在一个船夫身侧走着，无照无证、悄然穿过港区，与朋友们重新会合。

## X / X

The Desert / 沙漠

===Original===
We remember seeing once a lithographic print representing a Christmas legend of the Middle Ages, in which a detachment of the Heavenly Host﻿—big, ugly, wild-looking angels﻿—are pursuing, with sword and pike, a group of terror-stricken little devils. The idea in the picture produced such an impression that one wished to see the helpless, pitiful imps in heaven and the armed winged furies, their pursuers, in the other place. Now, as we go through the many pages of Shakib’s, in which he dilates of the mischances, the persecutions, and the flights of Khalid, and of which we have given an abstract, very brief but comprehensive, in the preceding Chapters, we are struck with the similarity in one sense between his *Dustur*-legend, so to speak, and that of the Middle Ages to which we have alluded. The devils in both pictures are distressing, pitiful; while the winged persecutors are horribly muscular, and withal atrociously armed.

Indeed, this legend of the Turkish angels of Fraternity and Equality, pursuing the Turkish little devils of reaction, so called, is most killing. But we can not see how the descendants of Yakut and Seljuk Khan, whether pursuers or pursued, whether *Dustur* winged furies they be, or Hamidian devils, are going to hold their own in face of the fell Dragon which soon or late must overtake them. That heavy, slow-going, slow-thinking Monster﻿—and it makes little difference whether he comes from the North or from the West﻿—will wait until the contending parties exhaust their strength and then﻿—but this is not our subject. We would that this pursuing business cease on all sides, and that everybody of all parties concerned pursue rather, and destroy, the big strong devil within them. Thus sayeth the preacher. And thus, for once, we, too. For does not every one of these furious angels of Equality, whether in Constantinople, in Berlin, in Paris, in London, or in New York, sit on his wings and reveal his horns when he rises to power? We are tired of wings that are really nothing but horns, misshaped and misplaced.

Look at our French-swearing, whiskey-drinking Tataric angels of the *Dustur*! Indeed, we rejoice that our poor little Devil is now beyond the reach of their dripping steel and rickety secondhand gibbets. And yet, not very far; for if the British Government consent or blink, Khalid and many real reactionists whom Cairo harbours, would have to seek an asylum elsewhere. And the third flight might not be as successful as the others. But none such is necessary. On the sands of the Libyan desert, not far from Cairo and within wind of Helwan, they pitch their tents. And Mrs. Gotfry is staying at al-Hayat, which is a stone’s throw from their evening fire. She would have Khalid live there too, but he refuses. He will live with his cousin and Shakib for a while. He is captivated, we are told, by that little cherub of a babe. But this does not prevent him from visiting his friend the Bahaist Priestess every day and dining often with her at the Hotel.

===Chinese===
我们记得曾见过一幅石版画，画的是中世纪的一则圣诞传说：一队天军——那些高大、丑陋、面目狰狞的天使——正挥着刀剑与长矛，追赶一群吓破了胆的小魔鬼。这幅画的立意给人留下的印象如此之深，以至于人巴不得让那些无助而可怜的小鬼们升入天堂，却把那些执兵甲、生羽翼的怒灵——他们的追赶者——打发到「那另一个所在」去。如今，当我们翻阅沙基布那一卷又一卷的篇幅——他在其中铺陈哈立德种种的厄运、逼迫与逃亡，而这些我们在前数章已做过提要，虽极简略却颇赅备——我们不禁为其中某种意义上的相似而触动：他的「杜斯图尔（Dustur）」传说，不妨这样说，竟与我们方才所提及的那则中世纪传说颇为相近。两幅画里的魔鬼都那般凄苦可怜；而那些生着翅膀的追逼者，却又肌肉暴突得骇人，且全副武装、凶焰万丈。

诚然，这则所谓「博爱」与「平等」的土耳其天使、追逼着所谓「反动派」的土耳其小魔鬼的传说，最是令人发噱。然而我们看不出，雅库特（Yakut）与塞尔柱汗（Seljuk Khan）的子孙——无论其为追赶者抑或被赶者，无论其为杜斯图尔的生翼怒灵，抑或哈米德式的魔鬼——在那条迟早必将追上他们的凶龙面前，将如何自存。那头笨重、行动迟缓、心思迟钝的巨怪——而它从北方来、抑或从西方来，并无多大分别——会一直候着，等到相争的双方力竭之时，然后……但这并非我们的题目。我们但愿这追赶的营生能从四面八方一并止息，而所有党派的所有人，宁可掉头去追赶、去扑灭各自心中那头又大又强的魔鬼。传道者如是说。而这一回，我们亦如是说。因为那些「平等」的怒焰天使——无论在君士坦丁堡、在柏林、在巴黎、在伦敦、抑或在纽约——岂不一朝大权在握，便都坐到自己的双翼之上、露出本来的犄角么？我们早已厌倦了那些不过是犄角、却畸形而错位的翅膀了。

且看我们那些满口法文脏话、豪饮威士忌的杜斯图尔的鞑靼式天使罢！诚然，我们庆幸我们那可怜的小魔鬼如今已逃出他们那淋漓的刀锋与那些摇摇欲坠的二手绞架所能及之处。然而也并不很远；因为一旦英国政府首肯、抑或睁一只眼闭一只眼，哈立德以及开罗所庇护的许多真正的反动派，便不得不到别处去另觅避难之所。而这第三度的逃亡，未必能像前几回那般顺遂。但这样的逃亡已无必要。在利比亚沙漠的沙地上，离开罗不远、在赫尔万（Helwan）的风声可闻之处，他们扎下了营。而戈特弗里夫人（Mrs. Gotfry）正下榻于海亚特（al-Hayat）——那儿离他们夜炊之火不过一箭之遥。她本要哈立德也住到那里去，可他不肯。他要与他的表妹以及沙基布同住一阵子。据我们所知，他已被那个小天使般的婴孩迷住了心窍。但这并不妨碍他每日去探访他的朋友那位巴哈伊女祭司（Bahaist Priestess），并常常与她在旅邸共进晚餐。

===Original===
She, too, not infrequently comes to the camp. Indeed, finding the solitude agreeable she has a tent pitched near theirs. And as a relief from the noise and bustle of tourists and the fatiguing formalities of Hotel life, she repairs thither for a few days every week.

Now, in this austere delicacy of the desert, where allwhere is the softness of pure sand, Khalid is perfectly happy. Never did he seem so careless, our Scribe asserts, and so jovial and childlike in his joys. Far from the noise and strife of politics, far from the bewildering tangle of thought, far from the vain hopes and dreams and ambitions of life, he lives each day as if it were the last of the world. Here are joys manifold for a weary and persecuted spirit: the joy of having your dearest friend and comrade with you; the joy of nursing and helping to restore to health and happiness the woman dearest to your heart; the joy of a Love budding in beauty and profusion; and﻿—this, the rarest and sublimest for Khalid﻿—the joy of worshipping at the cradle﻿—of fondling, caressing, and bringing up one of the brightest, sweetest, loveliest of babes.

Najib is his name﻿—it were cruel to neutralise such a prodigy﻿—and he is just learning to walk and lisp. Khalid teaches him the first step and the first monosyllable, receiving in return the first kiss which his infant lips could voice. With what joy Najib makes his first ten steps! With what zest would he practise on the soft sands, laughing as he falls, and rising to try again. And thus, does he quickly, wonderfully develop, unfolding in the little circle of his caressers﻿—in his mother’s lap, in Shakib’s arms, on Khalid’s back, on Mrs. Gotfry’s knee﻿—the irresistible charm of his precocious spirit.

===Chinese===
她，也不时来到营中。诚然，她发觉这清寂之地颇惬心意，便在他们营帐近旁另支了一顶帐篷。而作为对游客的喧嚣扰攘、旅邸生活那令人疲惫的繁文缛节的一种调剂，她每周总要到那里去消磨几日。

如今，在这沙漠那素朴而精微的意境之中——四下里无处不是那纯净柔沙的绵软——哈立德可谓幸福之至。我们的抄写人断言，他从未见过哈立德如此无忧无虑，如此在其乐事之中那般欢快而天真。远离政治的喧嚣与纷争，远离思想的纷乱纠葛，远离人生那虚妄的希望、梦想与野心，他把每一个日子都过得如同这世界的末日一般。此处有一个疲倦而受逼迫的灵魂所能享的种种欢乐：有你最亲爱的朋友与同志相伴之乐；有看护、并助你心头最珍视的女子重获健康与幸福之乐；有一段在美与丰盛之中萌发的爱情之乐；以及——这对哈立德而言最稀有、最崇高的——在摇篮前膜拜之乐——即抚弄、怜惜、并抚养一个最明慧、最甜美、最可爱的小婴孩之乐。

纳吉布（Najib）是他的名字——把这样一个神童加以「中性化」未免太残忍——他刚刚在学步、学语。哈立德教他迈出第一步、吐出第一个单音字，而所得的回报，便是那双婴唇所能发出的第一个吻。纳吉布迈出他最初的十步时，是带着何等的欢欣！他在柔软的沙地上练习得何等起劲——摔倒了便笑，爬起来再试。就这样，他发育得又快又奇妙，在那小小一圈怜爱他的人中间——在母亲的膝上、在沙基布的怀里、在哈立德的背上、在戈特弗里夫人的膝头——渐渐展露出他那早慧之灵那不可抗拒的魅力。

===Original===
In two months of desert life, Najib could run on the sands and sit down when tired to rest; in two months he could imitate in voice and gesture whatever he heard or saw: the donkey’s bray, and with a tilt of the head like him; the cry of the cock; the shrill whistle of the train; and the howling of donkey boys. His keen sense of discrimination in sounds is incredible. And one day, seeing a Mohammedan spreading his rug to pray, he begins to kneel and kiss the ground in imitation of him. He even went into the tent and brought Khalid’s jubbah to spread it on the sand likewise for that purpose. So sensitive to outside impressions is this child that he quickly responds to the least suggestion and with the least effort. Early in the morning, when the chill of night is still on the sands, he toddles into Khalid’s tent cooing and warbling his joy. A walking jasmine flower, a singing ray of sunshine, Khalid calls him. And the mother, on seeing her child thus develop, begins to recuperate. In this little garden of happiness, her hope begins to blossom.

But Khalid would like to know why Najib, on coming into his tent in the morning and seeing him naked, always pointed with his little finger and with questioning smile, to what protruded under the navel. The like questions Khalid puts with the ease and freedom of a child. And writes full pages about them, too, in which he only succeeds in bamboozling himself and us. For how can we account for everything a child does? Even the psychologist with his reflex-action theory does not solve the whole problem. But Khalid would like to know﻿—and perhaps not so innocently does he dwell upon this subject as upon others﻿—he would like to know the significance of Najib’s pointed finger and smile. It may be only an accident, Khalid. “But an accident,” says he, “occurring again and again in the same manner under stated conditions ceases to be such.” And might not the child, who is such an early and keen observer, have previously seen his mother in native buff, and was surprised to see that appendage in you, Khalid?

Even at al-Hayat Najib is become popular. Khalid often comes here carrying him on his back. And how ready is the child to salaam everybody, and with both hands, as he stands on the veranda steps. “Surely,” says Khalid, “there is a deeper understanding between man and child than between man and man. For who but a child dare act so freely among these polyglots of ceremony in this little world of frills and frocks and feathers? Who but a child dare approach without an introduction any one of these solemn-looking tourists? Here then is the divine source of the sweetest and purest joy. Here is that one touch of Nature which makes the whole world kin. For the child, and though he be of the lowest desert tribe, standing on the veranda of a fashionable Hotel, can warm and sweeten with the divine flame that is in him, the hearts of these sour-seeming, stiff-looking tourists who are from all corners of the earth. Is not this a miracle? My professor of psychology will say, ‘Nay.’ But what makes the heart leap in that grave and portly gentleman, who might be from Finland or Iceland, for all I know, when Najib’s hand is raised to him in salutation? What makes that stately and sombre-looking dame open her arms, when Najib plucks a flower and, after smelling it, presents it to her? What makes that reticent, meditative, hard-favoured ancient, who is I believe a psychologist, what makes him so interested in observing Najib when he stands near the piano pointing anxiously to the keyboard? For the child enjoys not every kind of music: play a march or a melody and he will keep time, listing joyously from side to side and waving his hand in an arch like a maestro; play something insipid or chaotic and he will stand there impassive as a statue.”

===Chinese===
在沙漠生活了两个月，纳吉布便能在沙上奔跑，疲倦时还会自己坐下歇息；两个月里，他已能以声以态模仿他所闻所见的一切：驴子的嘶鸣——并且像驴那样把头一歪；雄鸡的啼叫；火车的尖厉汽笛声；以及赶驴孩子们的吆喝。他对声音的敏锐辨别力，简直令人难以置信。一日，他见一位穆斯林（Mohammedan）铺开跪毯要礼拜，便也学着他的样，跪下来亲吻土地。他甚至走进帐篷，把哈立德的那件阿拉伯长袍（jubbah）拖出来，也同样铺在沙地上，作此用途。这孩子对外来的印象如此敏感，以至于他只需极微的提示、极少的力气，便能迅速作出回应。清晨，当夜的寒意犹留在沙上之时，他便蹒跚地走进哈立德的帐篷，咕咕哝哝、嘤嘤呀呀地唱出他的欢喜。一朵会走的茉莉花，一缕会歌唱的阳光——哈立德这样唤他。而那位母亲，看着自己的孩子这般成长，也开始恢复了元气。在这一小片幸福的园地里，她的希望开始绽放。

但是哈立德却想知道，何以纳吉布清早走进他的帐篷、看见他赤身裸体之时，总要用那根小指头、带着疑问的微笑，指着肚脐底下那个突出的东西。这一类问题，哈立德总是带着孩童般的从容与无拘无束地提出来。他还就这些问题写下整整几页，结果不过是把他自己、也把我们绕得云里雾里。因为孩子的一举一动，我们又怎能事事都给出一个交代？便是那位心理学家，凭他那套「反射动作」之说，也不能把整个问题解答清楚。但哈立德偏要想个明白——而且他在这个题目上所盘桓的，也许并不像在别的题目上那样天真——他想知道纳吉布那一指、那一笑，究竟意味着什么。那也许不过是一次偶然罢，哈立德。「可是，」他说，「一桩偶然之事，倘若在既定条件下再三再四地以同样方式发生，它便不再是偶然了。」再者，这孩子既是个如此早慧而敏锐的观察者，难道他先前不曾见过他母亲的一丝不挂之态，因而才惊讶地发觉你哈立德身上竟长着那样一件附属物么？

便是在海亚特，纳吉布也成了众人所宠爱的人物。哈立德常常背着他来到这里。而这孩子站在游廊的台阶上，是何等热切地向每一个人行色兰（salaam）之礼——并且是双手齐上。「诚然，」哈立德说，「人与孩童之间的理解，比人与人之间的更深。因为在这满是褶边、裙裾与羽毛的小小世界里，在这群操着种种语言的礼数之客中间，除了一个孩童，谁还敢如此无拘无束地行动？除了一个孩童，谁还敢不经人引见，便径直走近这些神色肃穆的游客中的任何一位？故而此处便有着那最甜美、最纯净之乐的神圣源头。此处便有那「一点天性之触」，使得整个世界皆为一家。因为这孩子——哪怕他出身于沙漠中最低微的部族——站在一座时髦旅邸的游廊之上，也能以他心中那一点神圣的火焰，去温暖、去甘甜化这些看似冷峻、神色僵硬、来自天涯海角的游客们的心。这岂不是一个奇迹？我那位心理学教授定会说：『否。』然而，当纳吉布举手向他致意之时，是什么使那位——据我所知也许来自芬兰或冰岛——那位庄严而魁梧的先生心头一跃？是什么使那位雍容而面色沉郁的夫人，在纳吉布摘下一朵花、闻过之后再献给她之时，张开了双臂？又是什么使那位寡言、好思、面貌粗陋的老者——我相信他是一位心理学家——是什么使他如此兴致勃勃地观察着纳吉布，当那孩子站在钢琴边、焦急地指着琴键之时？因为这孩子并非什么音乐都喜欢：奏一支进行曲或一段旋律，他便合着拍子，欢快地左右摇摆，把手在空中划出一道拱形，活像一位指挥家（maestro）；奏一段乏味或混乱的东西，他便如一尊雕像那般木然站在那里。」

===Original===
And “the reticent hard-favoured ancient,” who turns out to be an American professor of some ology, explains to Khalid why lively music moves children, while soft and subtle tones do not. But Khalid is not open to argument on the subject. He prefers to believe that children, especially when so keenly sensitive as his prodigy, understand as much, if not more, about music as the average operagoer of today. But that is not saying much. The professor furthermore, while admitting the extreme precocity of Najib’s mind, tries to simplify by scientific analysis what to Khalid and other laymen seemed wonderful, almost miraculous. Here, too, Khalid botches the arguments of the learned gentleman in his effort to give us a summary of them, and tells us in the end that never after, so long as that professor was there, did he ever visit al-Hayat.

He prefers to frolic and philosophise with his prodigy on the sands. He goes on all four around the tent, carrying Najib on his back; he digs a little ditch in the sand and teaches him how to lie therein. Following the precept of the Greek philosophers, he would show him even so early how to die. And Najib lies in the sand-grave, folds his hands on his breast and closes his eyes. Rising therefrom, Khalid would teach him how to dance like a dervish, and Najib whirls and whirls until he falls again in that grave.

When Mrs. Gotfry came that day, Khalid asked the child to show her how to dance and die, and Najib begins to whirl like a dervish until he falls in the grave; thereupon he folds his arms, closes his eyes, and smiles a pathetic smile. This by far is the masterpiece of all his feats. And one evening, when he was repeating this strange and weird antic, which in Khalid’s strange mind might be made to symbolise something stranger than both, he saw, as he lay in the grave, a star in the sky. It was the first time he saw a star; and he jumped out of his sand-grave exulting in the discovery he had made. He runs to his mother and points the star to her.﻿ ﻿…

And thus did Khalid spend his halcyon months in the desert. Here was an arcadia, perfect but brief. For his delight in infant worship, and in the new Love which was budding in beauty and profusion, and in tending his sick cousin who was recovering her health, and in the walks around the ruins in the desert with his dearest comrade and friend﻿—these, alas, were joys of too pure a nature to endure.

===Chinese===
而那位「寡言、面貌粗陋的老者」——原来他是美国某门「某某学」的教授——便向哈立德解释，何以活泼的乐音能打动孩童，而轻柔幽微的声调却不能。但哈立德在这个题目上却是不肯听人讲理的。他宁可相信：孩童——尤其像他这位神童一般敏锐的孩童——对音乐所领悟的，即便不超过今日普通的歌剧常客，也不在他们之下。不过这话也算不得多高。再者，那位教授虽然承认纳吉布的心智早熟至极，却试图以科学分析去化简那些在哈立德与其他门外汉看来奇妙、近乎神迹的事物。在这里，哈立德也是欲为我们综述那位博学先生的论点，却把他的论点搅得一塌糊涂，末了还告诉我们：自此之后，只要那位教授还在，他便再也不踏进海亚特一步。

他宁可与他那位神童在沙地上嬉戏、玄想。他绕着帐篷四肢着地地爬，背上驮着纳吉布；他在沙里掘出一条小沟，教他如何躺在其中。循着希腊哲学家的遗训，他要趁这么早便教他如何去死。于是纳吉布便躺在那沙坟之中，双手交叠于胸前，闭上双眼。从坟中起来之后，哈立德又教他如何像一位德尔维希（dervish）那样起舞，纳吉布便一旋再旋，直旋到再度跌进那座坟里。

那日戈特弗里夫人到来之时，哈立德便请那孩子向她表演如何起舞、如何去死；纳吉布便如德尔维希一般旋转起来，直旋到跌进坟里；随即他交起双臂、阖上双眼，露出一个凄婉的微笑。这一手，远胜他一切绝技，可谓登峰造极。某晚，当他又在重复这古怪而诡异的把戏——这在哈立德那奇特的心思中，未尝不可象征比两者都更奇异的什么——他躺在坟里之时，望见了天上的一颗星。这是他头一回看见一颗星；他便从那沙坟里一跃而起，为自己这一发现而欢跃不已。他奔向母亲，把那颗星指给她看。……

哈立德便这样在沙漠里度过了他那宁馨的数月。此处曾是一片阿卡狄亚（Arcadia）——完美然而短暂。因为他在婴孩膜拜之中所得的欢欣，在那段于美与丰盛之中萌发的新爱情里所得的欢欣，在看护他那位正康复的病表妹时所得的欢欣，在与他最亲爱的同志兼友伴一同漫步于沙漠废墟之间时所得的欢欣——唉，这些都是太纯粹了的欢乐，难以长久。

## Al-Khatimah / 终结章

===Original===
"But I can not see all that you see."

"Then you do not love me."

"Back again to Swedenborg—I told you more than once that he is not my apostle."

"Nor is he mine. But he has expressed a great truth, Jamilah. Now, can you love me in the light of that truth?"

"You are always asking me that same question, Khalid. You do not understand me. I do not believe in marriage. I tried it once; I will not try it again. I am married to Bahaism. And you Khalid—remember my words—you will yet be an apostle—the apostle—of Bahaism. And you will find me with you, whether you be in Arabia, in America, or in Egypt. I feel this—I know it—I am positive about it. Your star and mine are one. We are born under the same star. We are now in the same orbit, approaching the same nadir. We are ruled by our stars. I believe this, and you don't. At least, you say you don't. But you do. You don't know your own mind. The trend of the current of your life is beyond your grasp, beyond your comprehension. I know. And you must listen to me. You must follow my advice. If you can not come with me now to the States, you will await me here. I am called on a pressing business. And within three months, at the most, I shall return and find you waiting for me right here, in this desert."

"I can not understand you."

"You will yet."

"But why not try to understand me? Can you not find in my ideas the very essence of Bahaism? Can you not come up to my height and behold there the star that you have taken for your guide? My Truth, Jamilah, can you not see that? Love and Faith, free from all sectarianism and all earthly authority—what is Bahaism or Mohammedanism or Christianity beside them? Moreover, I have a mission. And to love me you must believe in *me*, not in the Baha. You laugh at my dream. But one day it will be realised. A great Arab Empire in the borderland of the Orient and Occident, in this very heart of the world, this Arabia, this Egypt, this Field of the Cloth of Gold, so to speak, where the Male and Female of the Spirit shall give birth to a unifying faith, a unifying art, a unifying truth—"

"Vagaries, chimeras," interrupted Mrs. Gotfry. "Bahaism is established, and it needs a great apostle. It needs you; it will have you. I will have you. Your destiny is interwoven with mine. You can not flee it, do what you may. We are ruled by our stars, Khalid. And if you do not realise this now, you will realise it tomorrow. Here, give me your hand."

"I can not."

"Very well, then. Goodbye—au revoir. In three months you will change your mind. In three months I will return to the East and find you waiting for me, even here in this desert. Think on it, and take care of yourself. Au revoir."

===Chinese===
「可我看不见你所看见的那一切。」

「那么你便不爱我。」

「又绕回斯韦登堡（Swedenborg）了——我跟你说过不止一回，他不是我的使徒。」

「他也并非我的使徒。可他的确道出了一桩伟大的真理，贾米拉（Jamilah）。那么，你能否在这真理的光照之下来爱我？」

「你总在问我这同一个问题，哈立德。你不了解我。我不信婚姻这一套。我试过一次；我不想再试第二次。我已经和巴哈伊教（Bahaism）结了婚。而你，哈立德——记住我的话——你终将成为一名使徒——那名使徒——巴哈伊教的使徒。你将发觉我始终与你同在，无论你在阿拉伯、在美洲，还是在埃及。我感觉得到——我知道——我对此深信不疑。你的星和我的星，原是同一颗。我们都生在同一颗星之下。我们此刻走在同一条轨道上，趋近同一个天底。我们受我们的星辰主宰。我相信这一点，而你不信。至少，你说你不信。可你心里是信的。你不识得你自己的心。你生命潜流的趋向，超出了你的把握，超出了你的领会。我知道。所以你必须听我的。你必须听从我的劝告。你若此刻不能与我同赴合众国，那你便在此处等我。我有一桩急务被唤去。至多三个月，我便回来，发觉你就在此地、在这沙漠里，等着我。」

「我不明白你。」

「你终会明白的。」

「可为什么你不试着来明白我呢？你难道不能从我的思想里看出巴哈伊教最精微的精髓？你难道不能攀上我的高度，在那里瞻望你所奉为向导的那颗星？我的真理，贾米拉，你看不见么？爱与信，摆脱了一切宗派之见、摆脱了一切尘世权威——巴哈伊教也罢，伊斯兰教也罢，基督教也罢，在它们面前又算得了什么？况且，我负有一项使命。而你要爱我，就必须信的是*我*，不是巴哈（the Baha）。你笑我的梦。可终有一日，它要成为现实。一个伟大的阿拉伯帝国，矗立在东方与西方的交界之地，在这世界的心脏里——这阿拉伯，这埃及，这，不妨说，黄金布之野——在那里面，灵性之雄与灵性之雌将孕育出一种统一的信仰、一种统一的艺术、一种统一的真理——」

「痴人说梦、空中楼阁，」戈特弗里夫人（Mrs. Gotfry）打断他道。「巴哈伊教已经立定了，它正需要一位伟大的使徒。它需要你；它必将拥有你。我也必将拥有你。你的命运与我的交织在一起。随你怎样挣扎，你逃不掉的。我们受我们的星辰主宰，哈立德。你此刻即便识不破这一点，明日你总要识破的。来，把你的手给我。」

「我不能。」

「那便作罢。再会——后会有期。三个月之内，你会改主意的。三个月之内，我便回到东方，发觉你正等着我，便是在这沙漠里也行。你好好想想，自己保重。后会有期。」

===Original===
In this strange, mysterious manner, after pacing for hours on the sand in the sheen of the full moon, Mrs. Gotfry says farewell to Khalid.

He sits on a rock near his tent and ponders for hours. He seeks in the stars, as it were, a clue to the love of this woman, which he first thought to be unfathomable. There it is, the stars seem to say. And he looks into the sand-grave near him, where little Najib practises how to die. Yes; a fitting symbol of the life and love called modern, boasting of freedom. They dance their dervish dance, these people, even like Khalid's little Najib, and fall into their sand-graves, and fold their arms and smile: "We are in love—or we are out of it." Which is the same. No: he'll have none of this. A heart as simple as this desert sand, as deep in affection as this heaven, untainted by the uncertainties and doubts and caprices of modern life—only in such a heart is the love that endures, the love divine and eternal.

He goes into Najma's tent. The mother and her child are sound asleep. He stands between the bed and the cot contemplating the simplicity and innocence and truth, which are more eloquent in Najib's brow than aught of human speech. His little hand raised above his head seems to point to a star which could be seen through an opening in the canvas. Was it his star—the star that he saw in the sand-grave—the star that is calling to him?—

But let us resume our narration.

===Chinese===
就在这样一种奇异、神秘的方式里，在满月的光华之下、于沙上踱了几个钟头之后，戈特弗里夫人便这样向哈立德道了别。

他在自己帐篷旁的一块岩石上坐下，沉思了几个钟头。他在星辰之间——仿佛是——寻觅着解开这女子之爱的线索，他起初还以为这份爱深不可测。就在那里，星辰仿佛在说。他望向近旁那座沙坟，小小的纳吉布（Najib）正在其中演习如何去死。是的；这正是那所谓现代的、以自由自夸的生命与爱情的恰切象征。这些人跳着他们的德尔维希舞（dervish dance），一如哈立德的小纳吉布，然后落进他们的沙坟，抱起双臂，微笑道：「我们恋爱了——或者，我们不在恋爱了。」二者原是一回事。不；他不要这样的爱。一颗像这沙漠之沙一般质朴的心，像这天穹一般深情的心，未被现代生活里的种种游移、疑虑和任性所玷污——唯有在这样的心里，才有那长存的爱，那神圣而永恒的爱。

他走进娜吉玛（Najma）的帐篷。那母子二人正熟睡着。他站在床与小床之间，端详着那份纯朴、那份天真、那份真实——它们在小纳吉布的额头上，比任何人间的言语都更雄辩。他那只举过头顶的小手，仿佛正指着透过帆布缝隙可以望见的一颗星。那可是他的星——他在沙坟里看见的那颗星——那颗正在召唤他的星？——

可让我们接着叙述下去罢。

===Original===
A fortnight after Mrs. Gotfry's departure Shakib leaves the camp to live in Cairo. He is now become poet-laureate to one of the big pashas.

Khalid is left alone with Najma and Najib.

And one day, when they are playing a game of "donkey,"—Khalid carried Najib on his back, ran on all four around the tent, and Najma was the donkey-driver—the child of a sudden utters a shriek and falls on the sand. He is in convulsions; and after the relaxation, lo, his right hand is palsied, his mouth awry, and his eyes asquint. Khalid finds a young doctor at al-Hayat, and his diagnosis of the case does not disturb the mind. It is infantile paralysis, a disease common with delicate children. And the doctor, who is of a kind and demonstrative humour, discourses at length on the disease, speaks of many worse cases of its kind he cured, and assures the mother that within a month the child will recover. For the present he can but prescribe a purgative and a massage of the arm and spine. On the third visit, he examines the child's faeces and is happy to have discovered the seat and cause of the affection. The liver is not performing its function; and given such weak nerves as the child's, a torpid liver in certain cases will produce paralysis.

But Khalid is not satisfied with this. He places the doctor's prescription in his pocket, and goes down to Cairo for a specialist. He comes, this one, to disturb their peace of mind with his indecision. It is not infantile paralysis, and he can not yet say what it is. Khalid meanwhile is poring over medical books on all the diseases that children are heir to.

On the fifth day the child falls again in convulsions, and the left arm, too, is paralysed. They take him down to Cairo; and Medicine, considering the disease of his mother, guesses a third time—tuberculosis of the spine, it says—and guesses wrong. Again, considering the strabismus, the obliquity of the mouth, the palsy in the arms, and the convulsions, we guess closely, but ominously. Nay, Medicine is positive this time; for a fifth and a sixth Guesser confirm the others. Here we have a case of cerebral meningitis. That is certain; that is fatal.

===Chinese===
戈特弗里夫人离去之后两星期，沙基布（Shakib）离开营地，到开罗（Cairo）去住了。他如今已做了某位大帕夏（pasha）的桂冠诗人。

哈立德独自留下来，与娜吉玛和纳吉布在一起。

一日，他们正在玩一种「赶驴」的游戏——哈立德把纳吉布驮在背上，绕着帐篷四肢着地地跑，娜吉玛做那赶驴的——那孩子猛地发出一声尖叫，一头栽倒在沙上。他抽搐起来；抽搐过后，看哪，他的右手瘫痪了，嘴歪了，眼睛也斜了。哈立德在海亚特（al-Hayat）找到一位年轻医生，这人对病情的诊断倒不令人忧心。是小儿麻痹症，一种娇弱孩子常得的病。那医生为人热切、好做长篇大论，把这病讲了半天，说起许多他所治愈的、比这更重的同类病例，并向那位母亲保证，不出一个月孩子便会好。眼下他所能开的，不过是泻药一剂，再佐以手臂和脊柱的按摩。第三次上门时，他验了孩子的粪便，颇庆幸自己找到了病的所在与起因。是肝不行使它的职能了；以这孩子如此虚弱的神经，再配上一个怠工的肝，在某些情形下确会引发瘫痪。

可哈立德对此并不满意。他把医生的处方揣进口袋，亲自下开罗去请一位专科大夫。这一位来了，却用他的举棋不定搅扰了他们的安宁。这不是小儿麻痹症，他也还说不上究竟是什么。哈立德这边则埋头翻阅各种医书，查遍孩子们可能罹患的一切病症。

到了第五天，孩子又一次抽搐起来，左臂也跟着瘫痪了。他们把他带下开罗；这回医术——考虑到他母亲的病——第三次猜：脊柱结核，它说——又猜错了。再一回，综观那斜视、那口角歪斜、那双臂瘫痪与那抽搐，我们猜得近了，却也凶险。不，这回医术是确凿的了；因为第五位、第六位「猜测者」都附和了前面几位的判断。我们这里面对的是一桩脑膜炎。这是定局了；这是要命的。

===Original===
Najib is placed under treatment. They cut his hair, his beautiful flow of dark hair; rub his scalp with chloroform; keep the hot bottles around his feet, the ice bag on his head; and give him a spoon of physic every hour. "Make no noise around the room, and admit no light into it," further advises the doctor. Thus for two weeks the child languishes in his mother's arms; and resting from the convulsions and the coma, he would fix on Khalid the hollow, icy glance of death. No; the light and intelligence might never revisit those vacant eyes.

Now Shakib comes to suggest a consultation. The great English physician of Cairo, why not call *him*? It might not be meningitis, after all, and the child might be helped, might be cured.

The great guesswork Celebrity is called. He examines the patient and confirms the opinion of his confrères, rather his disciples.

"But the whole tissue," he continues with glib assurance, "is not affected. The area is local, and to the side of the ear that is sore. The strabismus being to the right, the affection must be to the left. And the pus accumulating behind the ear, under the bone, and pressing on the covering of the brain, produces the inflammation. Yes, pus is the cause of this." And he repeats the Arabic proverb in broken Arabic, "A drop of pus will disable a camel." Further, "Yes, the child's life can be saved by trepanning. It should have been done already, but the time's not passed. Let the surgeon come and make a little opening—no; a child can stand chloroform better than an adult. And when the pus is out he will be well."

In a private consultation the disciples beg to observe that there was no evidence of pus behind the ear. "It is beneath the skullbone," the Master asserts. And so we decide upon the operation. The Eye and Ear specialist is called, and after weighing the probabilities of the case and considering that the great Celebrity had said there was pus, although there be no evidence of it, he convinces Khalid that if the child is not benefited by the operation he cannot suffer from it more than he is suffering now.

The surgeon comes with his assistants. Little Najib is laid on the table; the chloroform towel is applied; the scalpels, the cotton, the basins of hot water, and other accessories, are handed over by one doctor to another. The Cutter begins. Shakib is there watching with the rest; Najma is in an adjacent room weeping; and Khalid is pacing up and down the hall, his brows moistened with the cold sweat of anguish and suspense.

No pus between the scalp and the bone: the little hammer and chisel are handed to the Cutter. One, two, three—the child utters a faint cry; the chloroform towel is applied again;—four, five, six, and the seventh stroke of the little hammer opens the skull. The Cutter then penetrates with his catheter, searches thoroughly through the brain—here—there—above—below—and finally holds the instrument up to his assistants to show them that there is—no pus! "If there be any," says he, "it is beyond the reach of surgery." The wound, therefore, is quickly washed, sewn up, and dressed, while everybody is wondering how the great Celebrity can be wrong.…

===Chinese===
纳吉布被施以治疗。他们剪去了他的头发——他那一头乌黑美丽的长发；用氯仿揉擦他的头皮；在他脚边围上热水瓶，在他头上敷冰袋；每一个钟头给他灌一勺药水。「屋里不许出声，也不许放进光亮，」医生又叮嘱道。就这样过了两个礼拜，孩子在母亲怀里一天天委顿下去；每从抽搐与昏迷中缓过一口气来，他便把那死亡般的、空洞而冰冷的眼光投向哈立德。不；那光亮与灵慧，也许再也不肯回到那一双空茫茫的眼睛里去了。

这时沙基布前来建议会诊。开罗那位大名鼎鼎的英国医师，何不请*他*来？毕竟，这也许并非脑膜炎，孩子也许还有救，也许还能治。

那位以猜测闻名的大人物被请来了。他诊视了病人，附和了他的同僚——不如说是他的门徒们——的意见。

「不过，」他接着以流利的自信说道，「整个组织并未受累。病区是局部的，就在那只发炎的耳朵那一侧。斜视既偏向右，病灶便必在左。脓液积聚于耳后、骨下，压迫着脑膜，遂引发炎症。是的，脓便是病根。」他又用蹩脚的阿拉伯语念了一句阿拉伯谚语：「一滴脓，便能废掉一头骆驼。」复又道：「是的，这孩子的命，可以靠环钻术救下。这事本该早做的，但时机尚未过去。叫外科大夫来，开一个小口——不；孩子比成人更能耐受氯仿。脓一排净，他便好了。」

私底下会诊时，门徒们小心指出：耳后并无脓的迹象。「脓在颅骨之下，」大师断言。于是我们决定动手术。眼耳专科大夫被请来了，他权衡了这病例的种种或然率，又念及那位大人物既说有脓——纵然并无迹象——便说服了哈立德：孩子若不能从这场手术获益，所受之苦也断不至于甚于他眼下的苦。

外科大夫带着他的助手来了。小纳吉布被放上手术台；蒙上氯仿面巾；手术刀、棉花、热水盆以及其他器具，由一位医生递给另一位。「主刀的」开始了。沙基布在那里与众同观；娜吉玛在隔壁房间里哭泣；而哈立德在厅里来回踱步，额头沁着痛苦与悬念的冷汗。

头皮与骨头之间并无脓：小锤与凿子递到了主刀者手中。一、二、三——孩子发出一声微弱的哭叫；氯仿面巾又蒙了上去；——四、五、六，第七记小锤敲开了颅骨。主刀者于是探入导液管，在脑中细细搜索——这里——那里——上方——下方——最后把器械举起来给助手们看：那里头——没有脓！「若有，」他说，「也在外科所及的范围之外了。」于是伤口被飞快洗净、缝合、包扎起来，而人人都在纳罕，那位大人物怎会错。……

===Original===
Little Najib remains under the influence of anaesthetics for two days—for two days he is in a trance. And on the third, the fever mounts to the danger line and descends again—only after he had stretched his little arm and breathed his last!

And Khalid and Najma and Shakib take him out to the desert and bury him in the sand, near the tent round which he used to play. There, where he stepped his first step, lisped his first syllable, smacked his first kiss, and saw for the first time a star in the heaven, he is laid; he is given to the Night, to the Eternity which Khalid does not fear. And yet, what tears, Shakib tells us, he shed over that little grave.

But about the time the second calamity approaches, when Najma begins to decline and waste away from grief, when the relapse sets in and carries her in a fortnight downward to the grave of her child, Khalid's eyes are as two pieces of flint stone on a sheet of glass. His tears flow inwardly, as it were, through his cracked heart.…

Like the poet Saadi, Khalid once sought to fill his lap with celestial flowers for his friends and brothers; and he gathered some; but, alas, the fragrance of them so intoxicated him that the skirt dropt from his hand.…

---

===Chinese===
小纳吉布一连两天处在麻醉之下——两天里他一直神志昏迷。到了第三天，热烧冲上危险线，又退将下来——却是在他伸出小臂、咽下最后一口气之后！

哈立德、娜吉玛和沙基布把他抱到沙漠里，埋在沙中，葬在他从前常绕着玩耍的那顶帐篷旁。在那里——他迈出第一步、咿呀出第一个音节、吧嗒出第一个亲吻、头一回看见天上的一颗星的地方——他被安放了；他被交托给黑夜，交托给那哈立德所不惧的永恒。然而，沙基布告诉我们，他在那座小小的坟前，流了多少泪啊。

可就在第二场灾殃逼近之际——当娜吉玛因悲伤而开始委顿、消瘦，当那次复发袭来、半个月里便把她向下拖去她孩子的坟边——哈立德的眼睛，却像两片燧石贴在一片玻璃之上。他的泪水，仿佛全向内里流去，流过他那裂开的心。……

像诗人萨迪（Saadi）一样，哈立德曾想为他的朋友与兄弟们，采满满一怀的天国之花；他也的确采到了几朵；可叹那花香将他醉得如此厉害，那兜满花的衣裾竟从他手中滑落了。……

---

===Original===

We are again at the Mena House, where we first met Shakib. And the reader will remember that the tears rushed to his eyes when we inquired of him about his Master and Friend. "He has disappeared some ten days ago," he then said, "and I know not whither." Therefore, ask us not, O gentle Reader, what became of him. How can *we* know? He might have entered a higher spiritual circle or a lower; of a truth, he is not now on the outskirts of the desert: deeper to this side or to that he must have passed. And passing he continues to dream of "appearance in the disappearance; of truth in the surrender; of sunrises in the sunset."

Now, fare *thee* well in either case, Reader. And whether well or ill spent the time we have journeyed together, let us not quarrel about it. For our part, we repeat the farewell words of Sheikh Taleb of Damascus: "Judge us not severely." And if we did not study to entertain thee as other Scribes do, it is because we consider thee, dear good Reader, above such entertainment as our poor resources can furnish, *Wassalmu aleik*!

In Freike which is in Mount Lebanon Syria on the twelfth day of January 1910 Anno Christi and the first day of Muharram 1328 Hegirah this Book of Khalid was finished

===Chinese===

我们又回到了米娜宫（Mena House），我们当初遇见沙基布的地方。读者想必还记得，我们向他问起他的师尊与挚友时，泪水是怎样涌上他眼眶的。「他已经失踪了大约十天了，」他那时说，「我不知道他去了哪里。」所以，温柔的读者啊，别再问我们他后来怎样了。我们又如何*知道*呢？他也许进了一个更高的灵性圈子，也许是一个更低的；说真的，他如今已不在沙漠的边沿上了：他必是更深地没入了这一边或那一边。而在行过之际，他继续做着梦——「在消隐之中见显现；在交托之中见真理；在日落之中见日出。」

那么，读者，无论结局如何，就此*别过*了。我们结伴同行的这段时光，无论花得值或不值，都不必为此争执。至于我们这一边，我们只把大马士革的塔列布长老（Sheikh Taleb of Damascus）那句道别的话再说一遍：「切莫苛责我们。」倘若我们不曾像别的抄写人那样，用心来取悦于你，那是因为，亲爱的好读者，我们把你看得比我们这点微薄家底所能供上的消遣更高，*愿平安归于你*（Wassalmu aleik）！

于叙利亚黎巴嫩山之弗里凯（Freike），基督纪元一九一〇年一月十二日、回历一三二八年穆哈兰月（Muharram）初一，《哈立德之书》告竣

## Al-Fatihah / 开端章

===Original===
In the Khedivial Library of Cairo, among the Papyri of the Scribe of Amen-Ra and the beautifully illuminated copies of the Koran, the modern Arabic Manuscript which forms the subject of this Book, was found. The present Editor was attracted to it by the dedication and the rough drawings on the cover; which, indeed, are as curious, if not as mystical, as ancient Egyptian symbols. One of these is supposed to represent a New York Skyscraper in the shape of a Pyramid, the other is a dancing group under which is written: "The Stockbrokers and the Dervishes." And around these symbols, in Arabic circlewise, these words:—"*And this is my Book, the Book of Khalid, which I dedicate to my Brother Man, my Mother Nature, and my Maker God.*"

Needless to say we asked at once the Custodian of the Library to give us access to this Book of Khalid, and after examining it, we hired an amanuensis to make a copy for us. Which copy we subsequently used as the warp of our material; the woof we shall speak of in the following chapter. No, there is nothing in this Work which we can call ours, except it be the Loom. But the weaving, we assure the Reader, was a mortal process; for the material is of such a mixture that here and there the raw silk of Syria is often spun with the cotton and wool of America. In other words, the Author dips his antique pen in a modern inkstand, and when the ink runs thick, he mixes it with a slabbering of slang. But we started to write an Introduction, not a Criticism. And lest we end by writing neither, we give here what is more to the point than anything we can say: namely, al-Fatihah, or the Opening Word of Khalid himself.

With supreme indifference to the classic Arabic proem, he begins by saying that his Book is neither a Memoir nor an Autobiography, neither a Journal nor a Confession.

"Orientals," says he, "seldom adventure into that region of fancy and fabrication so alluring to European and American writers; for, like the eyes of houris, our vanity is soft and demure. This then is a book of travels in an impalpable country, an enchanted country, from which we have all risen, and towards which we are still rising. It is, as it were, the chart and history of one little kingdom of the Soul—the Soul of a philosopher, poet and criminal. I am all three, I swear, for I have lived both the wild and the social life. And I have thirsted in the desert, and I have thirsted in the city: the springs of the former were dry; the water in the latter was frozen in the pipes. That is why, to save my life, I had to be an incendiary at times, and at others a footpad. And whether on the streets of knowledge, or in the open courts of love, or in the parks of freedom, or in the cellars and garrets of thought and devotion, the only *saki* that would give me a drink without the asking was he who called himself Patience.…

"And so, the Book of Khalid was written. It is the only one I wrote in this world, having made, as I said, a brief sojourn in its civilised parts. I leave it now where I wrote it, and I hope to write other books in other worlds. Now understand, Allah keep and guide thee, I do not leave it here merely as a certificate of birth or death. I do not raise it up as an epitaph, a trade-sign, or any other emblem of vainglory or lucre; but truly as a propylon through which my race and those above and below my race, are invited to pass to that higher Temple of mind and spirit. For we are all tourists, in a certain sense, and this world is the most ancient of monuments. We go through life as those pugreed-solar-hatted-Europeans go through Egypt. We are pestered and plagued with guides and dragomans of every rank and shade;—social and political guides, moral and religious dragomans: a Tolstoy here, an Ibsen there, a Spencer above, a Nietzche below. And there thou art left in perpetual confusion and despair. Where wilt thou go? Whom wilt thou follow?"

===Chinese===
在开罗（Cairo）的赫迪夫图书馆（Khedivial Library）里，在阿蒙-拉（Amen-Ra）的抄写人所留下的纸草文书之间，在那些装帧华美的《古兰经》（Koran）抄本之间，发现了这部构成本书主题的现代阿拉伯文手稿。本编者之所以被它吸引，是因为封面上的题辞与那几幅粗率的图画；这些东西，纵不似古埃及的符箓那般神秘，却也一般地离奇。其中一幅，据说画的是一座金字塔形的纽约摩天楼；另一幅是一组翩翩起舞的人像，下面写着：「股票经纪人与德尔维希（dervish，即苏菲苦行僧）。」而在这些图案周围，用阿拉伯文环行写着一句话：——「*这是我的书，哈立德之书；我把它献给我的兄弟——人，献给我的母亲——自然，献给我的造物主——上帝。*」

不消说，我们当下便请图书馆的管理人让我们一睹这部《哈立德之书》，审阅之后，又雇了一位抄手为我们誊录了一份副本。这副本后来便被我们用作本书材料的经线；至于那纬线，且待下一章再说。是的，在这部书里，除了那架「织机」之外，没有什么东西能算是我们的。然而这「织造」的过程，敢向读者诸君保证，实在是一桩要命的营生；因为材料是这样一种混合物：叙利亚的生丝，处处都与美洲的棉花和羊毛拈在一起。换言之，作者把他那杆古旧的笔，蘸进一只新式的墨台；墨汁稠了，他便掺上一星半点的俚语。可我们起初要写的，是一篇导言，不是一篇批评。为免到头来两头落空、什么也写不成，我们索性在此处奉上比任何赘言都更切题的东西：那便是 al-Fatihah——哈立德他本人的「开端之辞」。

他对古典的阿拉伯式序言全然不屑一顾，开篇便说，他这部书既非回忆录，亦非自传；既非日记，亦非忏悔录。

「东方人，」他说，「很少闯进那片虚饰与编造的地带——那地带对欧美作家可是诱人得很；因为我们的虚荣，一如天园美女（houris）的眼波，是轻柔的、端穆的。那么，这便是一部游记，记的是在一个无形之国的游历——一个着了魔的国度，我们都从那里升起来，此刻仍朝着它升起来。它，不妨说，是灵魂那一个小小王国的舆图与史乘——一个既是哲人、又是诗人、又是罪人的灵魂。这三者我皆是，我起誓，因为我既过过荒野的生活，也过过群居的生活。我在沙漠里渴过，在城里也渴过：前者的泉眼是干的；后者的水冻在管子里。正因如此，为了活命，我有时不得不做纵火者，有时不得不做强人。而无论是在知识的长街，还是在爱情的敞庭，在自由的园林，还是在思想与虔诚的地窖和阁楼里，那唯一不用我开口讨、便会给我一口水喝的斟酒人（saki），只有那位自称「忍耐」的。……

「于是，《哈立德之书》就这样写成了。这是我在这个世上写的唯一一本书——如我所说，我不过在它的文明地带小住过一阵。如今我把它留在写它的地方，而我希望到别的世界去写别的书。现在你要明白——愿真主（Allah）保佑你、指引你——我把它留在这里，并非仅仅当作一张生卒证明。我竖起它，既不是作墓志铭，不是作幌子，也不是任何其他虚名与财利的标记；它实在是一道前门，我的种族——以及高于我的种族、低于我的种族的众人——都被邀请穿过它，走进那更高的心灵与精神的神殿。因为在某种意义上，我们都是游客，而这世界是最古老的一处遗迹。我们走过人生，正如那些被扒光了、戴着遮阳帽的欧洲人走过埃及一样。我们被各色各样、各等各品的向导和通译（dragoman）纠缠作弄、搅扰不休；——社会的和政治的向导，道德的和宗教的通译：这里一个托尔斯泰（Tolstoy），那里一个易卜生（Ibsen），上头一个斯宾塞（Spencer），下头一个尼采（Nietzsche）。于是你便被丢在无休无止的迷惘与绝望之中。你要往哪里去？你要跟随哪一位？」

===Original===
"Or wilt thou tarry to see the work of redemption accomplished? For Society must be redeemed, and many are the redeemers. The Cross, however, is out of fashion, and so is the Dona Dulcinea motive. Howbeit, what an array of Masters and Knights have we, and what a variety! The work can be done, and speedily, if we could but choose. Wagner can do it with music; Bakunin, with dynamite; Karl Marx, with the levelling rod; Haeckel, with an injection of protoplasmic logic; the Pope, with a pinch of salt and chrism; and the Packer-Kings of America, with pork and beef. What wilt thou have? Whom wilt thou employ? Many are the applicants, many are the guides. But if they are all going the way of Jahannam, the Beef-packer I would choose. For verily, a gobbet of beef on the way were better than canned protoplasmic logic or bottled salt and chrism.…

"No; travel not on a Cook's ticket; avoid the guides. Take up thy staff and foot it slowly and leisurely; tarry wherever thy heart would tarry. There is no need of hurrying, O my Brother, whether eternal Jahannam or eternal Jannat await us yonder. Come; if thou hast not a staff, I have two. And what I have in my Scrip I will share with thee. But turn thy back to the guides; for verily we see more of them than of the ruins and monuments. Verily, we get more of the Dragomans than of the Show. Why then continue to move and remove at their command?—Take thy guidebook in hand and I will tell thee what is in it.

"No; the time will come, I tell thee, when everyone will be his own guide and dragoman. The time will come when it will not be necessary to write books for others, or to legislate for others, or to make religions for others: the time will come when everyone will write his own Book in the Life he lives, and that Book will be his code and his creed;—that Life-Book will be the palace and cathedral of his Soul in all the Worlds."

===Chinese===
「——或者，你愿留步，看着那救赎的工程告成？因为社会必须被救赎，而救赎者多的是。只不过，十字架如今已经不时兴了，托娜·杜尔西内娅（Dona Dulcinea）的动机也已不时兴了。话虽如此，我们拥有何等壮观的一队宗师与骑士，品种又何等繁多！这工程是可以办成的，而且很快便能办成——只要我们能定得下心来挑选。瓦格纳（Wagner）能用音乐来办；巴枯宁（Bakunin），用炸药；卡尔·马克思（Karl Marx），用那根丈量平齐的标尺；海克尔（Haeckel），用一针原生质式的逻辑；教皇，用一撮盐和一点圣膏；而美洲的那些包装业大王，用猪肉和牛肉。你要哪一样？你要雇哪一位？应征者多的是，向导多的是。可他们若是都往火狱（Jahannam）那条路上去，那我倒宁愿选那位牛肉包装商。因为说真的，路上有一块牛肉垫垫肚子，总胜过罐装的原生质逻辑，或瓶装的盐与圣膏。……

「不；莫要坐库克旅行社（Cook's）的票去旅行；躲开那些向导。拄起你的杖，慢悠悠、从容容地走着；你心里要在哪里歇脚，便在哪里歇脚。不必赶路，我的兄弟，无论那边等候我们的是永恒的火狱，还是永恒的天园（Jannat）。来；你若没有杖，我有两根。我干粮袋里的东西，也与你共享。可是，把你的背朝着那些向导罢；因为说真的，我们看见他们的时间，比看见那些遗迹和古迹的时间还多。说真的，我们从那些通译嘴里受的，比从那景致本身得的多。那么，又何必一味听凭他们的号令搬来移去？——把你的导游手册拿在手里，我来告诉你那里面写着什么。

「不；那一天总会来的，我告诉你，那一天，人人都做自己的向导和通译。那一天总会来：再不必替别人写书，再不必替别人立法，再不必替别人造宗教：那一天总会来，人人都在自己所过的那一生里，写下自己的书，而那本书便是他的法典、他的信条；——那一本「生命之书」，将是他的灵魂在一切世界里、所住的宫殿与教堂。」
